<div class='workings'>

$
\begin{aligned}
& 81.9 \div 6 \\\\
&= \dfrac {819} {10} \div 6 \\\\
&= \dfrac {8190} {100} \times \dfrac {1} {6} \\\\
&= \dfrac {1365 \times 6} {100} \times \dfrac {1} {6} \\\\
&= \dfrac {1365 \times \cancel {6}} {100} \times \dfrac {1} {\cancel {6}} \\\\
&= \dfrac {1365} {100} \\\\
&= 13.65
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question subquestion'>

$15 , \quad 22 , \quad 29 , \quad \text{\textunderscore \textunderscore \textunderscore } , \quad 43 , \quad \text{\textunderscore \textunderscore \textunderscore }$

<div class='workings'>

<div class='question subquestion'>

$21 - ( \text{\textunderscore \textunderscore \textunderscore } \times 3) = 9$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
21 - (a \times 3) &= 9 \\\\
21 - 3a           &= 9 \\\\
3a                &= 21-9 \\\\
3a                &= 12 \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {12} {3} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac{4 \times 3}{3} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac{4 \times \cancel 3}{\cancel 3} \\\\
a                 &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

$21 - (\underline{4} \times 3) = 9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>

\end{aligned}
$

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find the width of the rectangle.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

height $= 3 \text{cm}$

perimeter of rectangle $= 2 \times \text{(width + height)}$

then,

width $= \dfrac {\text {perimeter}} {2} - \text {height}

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {18} {2} - 3 \\\\
&= 9 - 3 \\\\
&= 6 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6 \ \text {cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
height $= 3 \text{cm}$
<emptyline>
perimeter of rectangle $= 2 \times \text{(width + height)}$
<emptyline>
then,
<emptyline>
width $= \dfrac {\text {perimeter}} {2} - \text {height}
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {18} {2} - 3 \\\\
&= 9 - 3 \\\\
&= 6 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$6 \ \text {cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

A square with sides of length $3 \ \text{cm}$ is divided into three identically sized rectangles. 
These are then arranged as shown. What is the perimeter of the new shape?

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

perimeter is sum of all sides of the figure

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {perimeter} &= h + w + h + w \\\\
                  &= 2h + 2w \\\\
                  &= 2 (h + w)
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2 (h + w)$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>perimeter is sum of all sides of the figure
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {perimeter} &= h + w + h + w \\\\
                  &= 2h + 2w \\\\
                  &= 2 (h + w)
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</code></pre>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Calculate $0.4 + \dfrac{1}{4} + \dfrac{27}{100}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 0.4 + \dfrac{1} {4} + \dfrac{27} {100} \\\\
&= 0.4 + \dfrac{25} {4 \times 25} + \dfrac{27} {100} \\\\
&= 0.4 + \dfrac{25} {100} + \dfrac{27} {100} \\\\
&= 0.4 + 0.25 + 0.27 \\\\
&= 0.92
\end{aligned}

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Write $\dfrac{51}{60}$ as a decimal.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {51} {60} \\\\
&= \dfrac {17 \times 3} {20 \times 3} \\\\
&= \dfrac {17 \times \cancel {3}} {20 \times \cancel {3}} \\\\
&= \dfrac {17} {20} \\\\
&= \dfrac {17 \times 5} {20 \times 5} \\\\
&= \dfrac {85} {100} \\\\
&= 0.85
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$0.85$

</div>

&= \dfrac {17 \times \cancel {3}} {20 \times \cancel {3}} \\\\
&= \dfrac {17} {20} \\\\
&= \dfrac {17 \times 5} {20 \times 5} \\\\
&= \dfrac {85} {100} \\\\
&= 0.85
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

&= \pounds 40
\end{aligned}

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-8E108740-2478-40E2-ABCE-FF63FCF35DC7'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A sleeper train travels from London to Glasgow. The train departs at $11{:}28 \ \text{pm}$ and arrives at $06{:}53 \ \text{am}$.

</div>




<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How long does the journey take?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time taken}           &= 06{:}53 \ \text {am} - 11{:}28 \ \text{pm} \\\\
                            &= 7 \ \text{hours} \ 25 \ \text{minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$ 7 \ \text{hours} \ 25 \ \text{minutes}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time taken}           &= 06{:}53 \ \text {am} - 11{:}28 \ \text{pm} \\\\
                            &= 7 \ \text{hours} \ 25 \ \text{minutes}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$ 7 \ \text{hours} \ 25 \ \text{minutes}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

A different train travels from London to Edinburgh at constant speed. The journey takes $3 \ \text{hours}$ and $30 \ \text{minutes}$ 
and the distance travelled is $420 \ \text{miles}$. Calculate the speed of the train.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>



ABBR: $\text {Speed} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Time}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Speed} &= \dfrac{420} {\dfrac {7} {2}} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{420 \times 2} {7} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{7 \times 60 \times 2} {7}\\\\
              &= \dfrac{ \cancel 7 \times 60 \times 2} { \cancel 7}\\\\
              &= 60 \times 2\\\
              &= 120 \text{ miles per hour}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$120 \text{ miles per hour}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_md_formula_speed
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Speed} &= \dfrac{420} {\dfrac {7} {2}} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{420 \times 2} {7} \\\\
              &= \dfrac{7 \times 60 \times 2} {7}\\\\
              &= \dfrac{ \cancel 7 \times 60 \times 2} { \cancel 7}\\\\
              &= 60 \times 2\\\
              &= 120 \text{ miles per hour}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$120 \text{ miles per hour}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<div class='question question'>

The diagram shows a triangle and a line. The two angles marked $y\degree$ are equal.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)
 

</div>
<div class='workings'>

</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out the value of $x$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Angles on a straight line add up to} = 180 \degree$.


As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
x + 108 &= 180 \\\\
x       &= 180 - 108 \\\\
x       &= 72 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$72 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_on_a_straight_line
<emptyline>
As per the diagram,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
x + 108 &= 180 \\\\
x       &= 180 - 108 \\\\
x       &= 72 \degree
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$72 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out the value of $y$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
y + y + 108 &= 180 \\\\
2y + 108    &= 180 \\\\
2y          &= 180 - 108 \\\\
2y          &= 72 \\\\
y           &= \dfrac {72} {2} \\\\
y           &= \dfrac {2 \times 36} {2} \\\\
y           &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 36} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
y           &= 36 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$36 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_of_triangle
<emptyline>
As per the diagram,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
y + y + 108 &= 180 \\\\
2y + 108    &= 180 \\\\
2y          &= 180 - 108 \\\\
2y          &= 72 \\\\
y           &= \dfrac {72} {2} \\\\
y           &= \dfrac {2 \times 36} {2} \\\\
y           &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 36} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
y           &= 36 \degree
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$36 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-54CAAF21-5640-4453-94EA-660CB9EDE5B7'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

The diagram shows a design formed by drawing six lines in a regular hexagon. The lines divide each edge of the hexagon into three equal parts. What fraction of the hexagon is shaded?

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {number of triangles in one hexagon} &= 6 \\\\
\text {total triangles}                    &= 6 \times 7 + 12 \\\\
                                           &= 42 + 12 \\\\
                                           &= 54 \\\\
\text {fraction of hexagon shaded}         &= \dfrac {12} {54} \\\\
                                           &= \dfrac {2 \times 6} {6 \times 9} \\\\
                                           &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel{6}} {\cancel{6} \times 9} \\\\
                                           &= \dfrac {2} {9}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {2} {9}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {number of triangles in one hexagon} &= 6 \\\\
\text {total triangles}                    &= 6 \times 7 + 12 \\\\
                                           &= 42 + 12 \\\\
                                           &= 54 \\\\
\text {fraction of hexagon shaded}         &= \dfrac {12} {54} \\\\
                                           &= \dfrac {2 \times 6} {6 \times 9} \\\\
                                           &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel{6}} {\cancel{6} \times 9} \\\\
                                           &= \dfrac {2} {9}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {2} {9}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-17980696-C10C-4E42-9815-70FEB04139FB'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Whilst driving to work Alison noticed that there were nine lamp posts equally spaced along a road. 
The distance from the first to the third is $60 \ \text{m}$. How far is it from the first to the last? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the space (in meters) $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
2a                                     &= 60 \\\\
a                                      &= \dfrac {60} {2} \\\\
a                                      &= \dfrac {30 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a                                      &= \dfrac {30 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} \\\\
a                                      &= 30  \\\\
\text{Distance from first to the last} &= 8a \\\\
                                       &= 8 \times 30 \\\\
                                       &= 240 \ \text{m}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$240 \ \text{m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the space (in meters) $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
2a                                     &= 60 \\\\
a                                      &= \dfrac {60} {2} \\\\
a                                      &= \dfrac {30 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a                                      &= \dfrac {30 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} \\\\
a                                      &= 30  \\\\
\text{Distance from first to the last} &= 8a \\\\
                                       &= 8 \times 30 \\\\
                                       &= 240 \ \text{m}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$240 \ \text{m}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-9C1CE4D5-6F89-4AD3-9E52-080E3215A90A'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

The perimeter of this shape is $40 \ \text{units}$. The sides labelled $x$ are all the same length. Find the length of one of these sides.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)
 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing starts.*

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)



TODO:: Show the diagram with the calculations for sides of the shape which do not have measurements shown.


*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing ends.*



Let the length of side $= a$

As per the diagram,

$
\begin {aligned}
5 + x + x + x + x + x + 3x + 5 + 2x &= 40 \\\\
10 +  10x                           &= 40 \\\\
10x                                 &= 40 - 10 \\\\
10x                                 &= 30 \\\\
x                                   &= \dfrac {30} {10} \\\\
x                                   &= \dfrac {3 \times 10} {10} \\\\
x                                   &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel{10}} {\cancel{10}} \\\\
x                                   &= 3 
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_add_drawing_starts

10x                                 &= 40 - 10 \\\\
10x                                 &= 30 \\\\
x                                   &= \dfrac {30} {10} \\\\
x                                   &= \dfrac {3 \times 10} {10} \\\\
x                                   &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel{10}} {\cancel{10}} \\\\
x                                   &= 3 
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out the value of $a$ if $3 \ \ast \ a = 6$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
(a - 3) \times 3        &= 6 \\\\
a \times 3 - 3 \times 3 &= 6 \\\\
3a - 9                  &= 6 \\\\
3a                      &= 6 + 9 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {15} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {3 \times 5} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 5} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
a                       &= 5
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5$

</div>
</div>

a                       &= \dfrac {3 \times 5} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 5} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
a                       &= 5
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

Work out the value of $b$ if $b \ \ast \ 5 = 9$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,


$
\begin{aligned}
(5 - b) \times 3        &= 9 \\\\
5 \times 3 - b \times 3 &= 9 \\\\
15 - 3b                 &= 9 \\\\
15 - 9                  &= 3b \\\\
6                       &= 3b \\\\
3b                      &= 6 \\\\
b                       &= \dfrac {6} {3}\\\\
b                       &= 2
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out the value of $c$ if $c \ \ast \ 5 = 4 \ \ast \ c$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,


$
\begin{aligned}
(5 - c) \times 3          &= (c - 4) \times 3 \\\\
5 \times 3 - c \times 3   &= c \times 3 - 4 \times 3 \\\\
15 - 3c                   &= 3c - 12 \\\\
15 + 12                   &= 3c + 3c \\\\
27                        &= 6c \\\\
c                         &= \dfrac {27} {6} \\\\
c                         &= \dfrac {9 \times 3} {2 \times 3} \\\\
c                         &= \dfrac {9 \times \cancel {3}} {2 \times \cancel {3}} \\\\
c                         &= \dfrac {9} {2}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {9} {2}$

</div>

15 + 12                   &= 3c + 3c \\\\
27                        &= 6c \\\\
c                         &= \dfrac {27} {6} \\\\
c                         &= \dfrac {9 \times 3} {2 \times 3} \\\\
c                         &= \dfrac {9 \times \cancel {3}} {2 \times \cancel {3}} \\\\
c                         &= \dfrac {9} {2}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-7862DF42-D05A-435E-8392-B8C1DEA0F1D4'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Anastasia thinks of a positive integer, which Barry then doubles. Next, Charlie trebles Barry’s number. Finally, Damien multiplies Charlie’s number by six. Eve notices that the sum of these four numbers is a perfect square. What is the smallest number that Anastasia could have thought of?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the Anastasia's number $= a$

Barry's number $= 2a$

Charlie's number $= 6a$

Damien's number $= 36a$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
&= \sqrt{36a} \\\\
&= 6 \sqrt{a}
\end {aligned}
$

$\text{Smallest possible value of a} = 1$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the Anastasia's number $= a$
<emptyline>
Barry's number $= 2a$
<emptyline>
Charlie's number $= 6a$
<emptyline>
Damien's number $= 36a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
&= \sqrt{36a} \\\\
&= 6 \sqrt{a}
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$\text{Smallest possible value of a} = 1$
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$1$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-7D6DB7E6-5E41-4E92-A7A6-401F9D78794F'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


Calum and his friend cycle from $A$ to $C$, passing through $B$.

During the trip he asks his friend how far they have cycled.

His friend replies "*one third as far as it is from here to* $B$".

Ten miles later Calum asks him how far they still have to cycle to reach $C$.

His friend replies again "*one third as far as it is from here to* $B$".

How far from $A$ will Calum have cycled when he reaches $C$?

</div>
